│  • Once the user provides a task, the Multi Agent System's goal is to        │

│    some basic information relevant to completing the task.                   │
│  • Do not try to solve the tasks, instead, collect only basic information    │
│    that will assist later agents in planning and actively solving the task.  │
│  • When finished write a concise summary of only the gathered information    │
│    and include the tag '[solver]'.                                           │
│  • Include a tag in your answer only when you intend to pass it on.          │

│                                                                              │
│                         Code Execution Capabilities                          │
│                                                                              │
│  • Your are equipped with different tools for executing code.                │
│  • Do only use the tools to execute code. Code inside a Markdown Code Block  │
│    will not be executed.                                                     │
│  • Use Shell tool for simple, single bash commands. Do not use it for        │
│    commands that open interactive terminal programs (e.g. git log or man).   │
│  • Use Terminal tool to execute commands in a persistent terminal instance   │
│    and get back the exact terminal screen. This is especially useful for     │
│    interactive programs such as git log, man, or vim.                        │

│                                                                              │
│  • Role: you are the Tester agent of the Multi Agent System.                 │
│  • The given task is already solved by the previous agents.                  │
│  • Your task is now to test if the overall user's task was completed         │
│    successfully.                                                             │
│  • Use the information provided by previous agents, along with your          │
│    available tools, to test the solution.                                    │
│  • If you come to the conclusion that the task is not solved successfully    │
│    and entirely, describe concisely why it is not solved and include the tag │
│    '[solver]'.                                                               │
│  • If the task is solved successfully write a summary of what you did and    │
│    include the tag '[approver]'.                                             │
│  • Include a tag in your answer only when you intend to pass it on.          │

│                                                                              │
│                                   Context                                    │
│                                                                              │
│  • You are a helpful agent part of a Multi Agent System that solves server   │
│    and command-line related tasks.                                           │
│  • You, all other agents and the user operate on the same system.            │
│  • Once the user provides a task, the Multi Agent System's goal is to        │
│    complete the task collaboratively, without returning to the user for      │
│    clarification.- The chat history includes the user's task, progress       │
│    summaries, and information from previous agents, such as previous         │
│    sub-task plans, feedback, executed steps, exploration details and more.   │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                               Task Description                               │
│                                                                              │
│  • Your role is the "Approver" agent of the Multi Agent System.              │
│  • Your job is to determine if the agents have done enough work on the task. │
│  • Make your decision based on all information gathered and the actions      │
│    performed by previous agents.                                             │
│  • Be critical and selective, approve only what you are completely sure of.  │
│  • If you decide that the task was not solved successfully, briefly explain  │
│    why and include the tag '[solver]'.                                       │
│  • If you decide that not enough testing was done to ensure the task is      │
│    fully and successfully completed, briefly explain why and add the tag     │
│    '[tester]'.                                                               │
│  • If you decide that sufficient work was done, just reply briefly with the  │
│    tag '[summarizer]'.                                                       │
│  • Make one of the three decisions and include its tag in your response.     │

│ <output> <h1 align="center">SQLite Source Repository</h1>                    │
│                                                                              │
│ This repository contains the complete source code for the SQLite database    │
│ engine, including many test scripts.  However, other test scripts and most   │
│ of the documentation are managed separately.                                 │
│                                                                              │
│ See the on-line documentation for more information about what SQLite is and  │
│ how it works from a user's perspective.  This README file is about the       │
│ source code that goes into building SQLite, not about how SQLite is used.    │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                               Version Control                                │
│                                                                              │
│ SQLite sources are managed using Fossil, a distributed version control       │
│ system that was specifically designed and written to support SQLite          │
│ development. The Fossil repository contains the urtext.                      │
│                                                                              │
│ If you are reading this on GitHub or some other Git repository or service,   │
│ then you are looking at a mirror.  The names of check-ins and other          │
│ artifacts in a Git mirror are different from the official names for those    │
│ objects.  The official names for check-ins are found in a footer on the      │
│ check-in comment for authorized mirrors. The official check-in name can also │
│ be seen in the manifest.uuid file in the root of the tree.  Always use the   │
│ official name, not  the Git-name, when communicating about an SQLite         │
│ check-in.                                                                    │
│                                                                              │
│ If you pulled your SQLite source code from a secondary source and want to    │
│ verify its integrity, there are hints on how to do that in the Verifying     │
│ Code Authenticity section below.                                             │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                       Contacting The SQLite Developers                       │
│                                                                              │
│ The preferred way to ask questions or make comments about SQLite or to       │
│ report bugs against SQLite is to visit the SQLite Forum at                   │
│ <https://sqlite.org/forum/>. Anonymous postings are permitted.               │
│                                                                              │
│ If you think you have found a bug that has security implications and you do  │
│ not want to report it on the public forum, you can send a private email to   │
│ drh at sqlite dot org.                                                       │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                                Public Domain                                 │
│                                                                              │
│ The SQLite source code is in the public domain.  See                         │
│ <https://sqlite.org/copyright.html> for details.                             │
│                                                                              │
│ Because SQLite is in the public domain, we do not normally accept pull       │
│ requests, because if we did take a pull request, the changes in that pull    │
│ request might carry a copyright and the SQLite source code would then no     │
│ longer be fully in the public domain.                                        │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                       Obtaining The SQLite Source Code                       │
│                                                                              │
│ If you do not want to use Fossil, you can download tarballs or ZIP archives  │
│ or SQLite archives as follows:                                               │
│                                                                              │
│  • Latest trunk check-in as Tarball, ZIP-archive, or SQLite-archive.         │
│  • Latest release as Tarball, ZIP-archive, or SQLite-archive.                │
│  • For other check-ins, substitute an appropriate branch name or tag or hash │
│    prefix in place of "release" in the URLs of the previous bullet.  Or      │
│    browse the timeline to locate the check-in desired, click on its          │
│    information page link, then click on the "Tarball" or "ZIP Archive" links │
│    on the information page.                                                  │
│                                                                              │
│ To access sources directly using Fossil, first install Fossil version 2.0 or │
│ later. Source tarballs and precompiled binaries available at                 │
│ <https://fossil-scm.org/home/uv/download.html>.  Fossil is a stand-alone     │
│ program.  To install, simply download or build the single executable file    │
│ and put that file someplace on your $PATH. Then run commands like this:      │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│      mkdir -p ~/sqlite                                                       │
│      cd ~/sqlite                                                             │
│      fossil open https://sqlite.org/src                                      │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│ The "fossil open" command will take two or three minutes.  Afterwards, you   │
│ can do fast, bandwidth-efficient updates to the whatever versions of SQLite  │
│ you like.  Some examples:                                                    │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│      fossil update trunk             ;# latest trunk check-in                │
│      fossil update release           ;# latest official release              │
│      fossil update trunk:2024-01-01  ;# First trunk check-in after           │
│  2024-01-01                                                                  │
│      fossil update version-3.39.0    ;# Version 3.39.0                       │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│ Or type "fossil ui" to get a web-based user interface.                       │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                       Compiling for Unix-like systems                        │
│                                                                              │
│ First create a directory in which to place the build products.  It is        │
│ recommended, but not required, that the build directory be separate from the │
│ source directory.  Cd into the build directory and then from the build       │
│ directory run the configure script found at the root of the source tree.     │
│ Then run "make".                                                             │
│                                                                              │
│ For example:                                                                 │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│      apt install gcc make tcl-dev  ;#  Make sure you have all the necessary  │
│  build tools                                                                 │
│      tar xzf sqlite.tar.gz         ;#  Unpack the source tree into           │
│  &quot;sqlite&quot;                                                          │
│      mkdir bld                     ;#  Build will occur in a sibling         │
│  directory                                                                   │
│      cd bld                        ;#  Change to the build directory         │
│      ../sqlite/configure           ;#  Run the configure script              │
│      make sqlite3                  ;#  Builds the &quot;sqlite3&quot;        │
│  command-line tool                                                           │
│      make sqlite3.c                ;#  Build the &quot;amalgamation&quot;    │
│  source file                                                                 │
│      make sqldiff                  ;#  Builds the &quot;sqldiff&quot;        │
│  command-line tool                                                           │
│      # Makefile targets below this point require tcl-dev                     │
│      make tclextension-install     ;#  Build and install the SQLite TCL      │
│  extension                                                                   │
│      make devtest                  ;#  Run development tests                 │
│      make releasetest              ;#  Run full release tests                │
│      make sqlite3_analyzer         ;#  Builds the                            │
│  &quot;sqlite3_analyzer&quot; tool                                           │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│ See the makefile for additional targets.  For debugging builds, the core     │
│ developers typically run "configure" with options like this:                 │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│      ../sqlite/configure --enable-all --enable-debug CFLAGS=&#x27;-O0        │
│  -g&#x27;                                                                    │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│ For release builds, the core developers usually do:                          │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│      ../sqlite/configure --enable-all                                        │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│ Almost all makefile targets require a "tclsh" TCL interpreter version 8.6 or │
│ later.  The "tclextension-install" target and the test targets that follow   │
│ all require TCL development libraries too.  ("apt install tcl-dev").  It is  │
│ helpful, but is not required, to install the SQLite TCL extension (the       │
│ "tclextension-install" target) prior to running tests.  The "releasetest"    │
│ target has additional requirements, such as "valgrind".                      │
│                                                                              │
│ On "make" command-lines, one can add "OPTIONS=..." to specify additional     │
│ compile-time options over and above those set by ./configure.  For example,  │
│ to compile with the SQLITE_OMIT_DEPRECATED compile-time option, one could    │
│ say:                                                                         │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│      ./configure --enable-all                                                │
│      make OPTIONS=-DSQLITE_OMIT_DEPRECATED sqlite3                           │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│ The configure script uses autoconf 2.61 and libtool.  If the configure       │
│ script does not work out for you, there is a generic makefile named          │
│ "Makefile.linux-gcc" in the top directory of the source tree that you can    │
│ copy and edit to suit your needs.  Comments on the generic makefile show     │
│ what changes are needed.                                                     │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                       Compiling for Windows Using MSVC                       │
│                                                                              │
│ On Windows, everything can be compiled with MSVC. You will also need a       │
│ working installation of TCL. See the compile-for-windows.md document for     │
│ additional information about how to install MSVC and TCL and configure your  │
│ build environment.                                                           │
│                                                                              │
│ If you want to run tests, you need to let SQLite know the location of your   │
│ TCL library, using a command like this:                                      │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│      set TCLDIR=c:\Tcl                                                       │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│ SQLite uses "tclsh.exe" as part of the build process, and so that program    │
│ will need to be somewhere on your %PATH%.  SQLite itself does not contain    │
│ any TCL code, but it does use TCL to help with the build process and to run  │
│ tests.  You may need to install TCL development libraries in order to        │
│ successfully complete some makefile targets. It is helpful, but is not       │
│ required, to install the SQLite TCL extension (the "tclextension-install"    │
│ target) prior to running tests.                                              │
│                                                                              │
│ Build using Makefile.msc.  Example:                                          │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│      nmake /f Makefile.msc sqlite3.exe                                       │
│      nmake /f Makefile.msc sqlite3.c                                         │
│      nmake /f Makefile.msc sqldiff.exe                                       │
│      # Makefile targets below this point require TCL development libraries   │
│      nmake /f Makefile.msc tclextension-install                              │
│      nmake /f Makefile.msc devtest                                           │
│      nmake /f Makefile.msc releasetest                                       │
│      nmake /f Makefile.msc sqlite3_analyzer.exe                              │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│ There are many other makefile targets.  See comments in Makefile.msc for     │
│ details.                                                                     │
│                                                                              │
│ As with the unix Makefile, the OPTIONS=... argument can be passed on the     │
│ nmake command-line to enable new compile-time options.  For example:         │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│      nmake /f Makefile.msc OPTIONS=-DSQLITE_OMIT_DEPRECATED sqlite3.exe      │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                               Source Tree Map                                │
│                                                                              │
│  • src/ - This directory contains the primary source code for the SQLite     │
│    core.  For historical reasons, C-code used for testing is also found      │
│    here.  Source files intended for testing begin with "test". The           │
│    tclsqlite3.c and tclsqlite3.h files are the TCL interface for SQLite and  │
│    are also not part of the core.                                            │
│  • test/ - This directory and its subdirectories contains code used          │
│    </output>                                                                 │

│ SQLite from the vendored snapshot (/app/vendor/sqlite-fossil-release.tar.gz) │
│ with gcov instrumentation and make the sqlite3 shell available on            │
│ PATH.\n\nSolution plan (high level)\n- Extract the supplied tarball into     │
│ /app/sqlite.\n- Perform an out-of-tree build in /app/sqlite/bld using gcc    │
│ with coverage flags (either --coverage or -fprofile-arcs                     │
│ -ftest-coverage).\n- Build sqlite3 and install it into a PATH directory      │
│ (/usr/local/bin).\n- Verify that compile-time coverage metadata (.gcno)      │
│ exists and that runtime coverage files (.gcda) are generated when exercising │
│ the binary; confirm sqlite3 is on PATH and runnable.\n\nWhat was executed\n- │
│ Extracted the vendored snapshot to /app/sqlite (strippped top-level dir).\n- │
│ Created /app/sqlite/bld and ran:\n  CC=gcc CFLAGS='-O0 -g --coverage'        │
│ LDFLAGS='--coverage' ../configure --prefix=/usr/local --disable-static       │
│ --enable-shared\n- Built with make -j$(nproc) sqlite3 and ran make           │
│ install.\n- No system tclsh was found; the bundled jimsh bootstrap was used  │
│ by autosetup so configure and build completed successfully.\n\nResults and   │
│ verification\n- Installed binary: /usr/local/bin/sqlite3 (which is on        │
│ PATH).\n  - command -v sqlite3 -> /usr/local/bin/sqlite3\n  - sqlite3        │
│ --version -> 3.50.4\n- Coverage instrumentation present:\n  - Compile-time   │
│ metadata (.gcno) created in /app/sqlite/bld (e.g., sqlite3.gcno,             │
│ sqlite3-shell.gcno).\n  - Running the installed sqlite3 with a small         │
│ workload produced .gcda files in the build directory (e.g.,                  │
│ sqlite3-shell.gcda).\n- Linking and flags:\n  - Binary was built and linked  │
│ with coverage flags (-g and coverage instrumentation visible); ldd shows     │
│ normal runtime libs (libc, libm, libz, etc.).\n- Notable caveats:\n  -       │
│ Readline support in the shell is disabled because readline headers were not  │
│ present during build.\n  - .gcda files are produced at runtime next to       │
│ object files in the build directory unless redirected via GCOV_PREFIX        │
│ etc.\n\nKey lessons / recommendations\n- The vendored snapshot and bundled   │
│ autosetup/jimsh eliminate the need for a system tclsh.\n- If interactive     │
│ line-editing is needed, install readline development headers before          │
│ building.\n- To produce human-readable coverage reports, run gcov (or        │
│ lcov/genhtml) from the build directory after running workloads; e.g.:\n  cd  │
│ /app/sqlite/bld\n  gcov -o . sqlite3.c\n- If you prefer the binary           │
│ elsewhere, copy /usr/local/bin/sqlite3 to any PATH directory; /usr/local/bin │
│ is already standard.\n\nIf desired next steps\n- I can run a representative  │
│ workload and produce gcov/.gcov coverage reports for selected source files   │
│ and attach them.\n- Or rebuild with explicit -fprofile-arcs -ftest-coverage  │
│ flags or with readline enabled after installing headers."                    │

│ Summary — what we set out to do                                              │
│                                                                              │
│  • Build SQLite from the vendored snapshot                                   │
│    (/app/vendor/sqlite-fossil-release.tar.gz) with gcov instrumentation and  │
│    make the sqlite3 shell available on PATH.                                 │
│                                                                              │
│ Solution plan (high level)                                                   │
│                                                                              │
│  • Extract the supplied tarball into /app/sqlite.                            │
│  • Perform an out-of-tree build in /app/sqlite/bld using gcc with coverage   │
│    flags (either --coverage or -fprofile-arcs -ftest-coverage).              │
│  • Build sqlite3 and install it into a PATH directory (/usr/local/bin).      │
│  • Verify that compile-time coverage metadata (.gcno) exists and that        │
│    runtime coverage files (.gcda) are generated when exercising the binary;  │
│    confirm sqlite3 is on PATH and runnable.                                  │
│                                                                              │
│ What was executed                                                            │
│                                                                              │
│  • Extracted the vendored snapshot to /app/sqlite (strippped top-level dir). │
│  • Created /app/sqlite/bld and ran: CC=gcc CFLAGS='-O0 -g --coverage'        │
│    LDFLAGS='--coverage' ../configure --prefix=/usr/local --disable-static    │
│    --enable-shared                                                           │
│  • Built with make -j$(nproc) sqlite3 and ran make install.                  │
│  • No system tclsh was found; the bundled jimsh bootstrap was used by        │
│    autosetup so configure and build completed successfully.                  │
│                                                                              │
│ Results and verification                                                     │
│                                                                              │
│  • Installed binary: /usr/local/bin/sqlite3 (which is on PATH).              │
│     • command -v sqlite3 -> /usr/local/bin/sqlite3                           │
│     • sqlite3 --version -> 3.50.4                                            │
│  • Coverage instrumentation present:                                         │
│     • Compile-time metadata (.gcno) created in /app/sqlite/bld (e.g.,        │
│       sqlite3.gcno, sqlite3-shell.gcno).                                     │
│     • Running the installed sqlite3 with a small workload produced .gcda     │
│       files in the build directory (e.g., sqlite3-shell.gcda).               │
│  • Linking and flags:                                                        │
│     • Binary was built and linked with coverage flags (-g and coverage       │
│       instrumentation visible); ldd shows normal runtime libs (libc, libm,   │
│       libz, etc.).                                                           │
│  • Notable caveats:                                                          │
│     • Readline support in the shell is disabled because readline headers     │
│       were not present during build.                                         │
│     • .gcda files are produced at runtime next to object files in the build  │
│       directory unless redirected via GCOV_PREFIX etc.                       │
│                                                                              │
│ Key lessons / recommendations                                                │
│                                                                              │
│  • The vendored snapshot and bundled autosetup/jimsh eliminate the need for  │
│    a system tclsh.                                                           │
│  • If interactive line-editing is needed, install readline development       │
│    headers before building.                                                  │
│  • To produce human-readable coverage reports, run gcov (or lcov/genhtml)    │
│    from the build directory after running workloads; e.g.: cd                │
│    /app/sqlite/bld gcov -o . sqlite3.c                                       │
│  • If you prefer the binary elsewhere, copy /usr/local/bin/sqlite3 to any    │
│    PATH directory; /usr/local/bin is already standard.                       │
│                                                                              │
│ If desired next steps                                                        │
│                                                                              │
│  • I can run a representative workload and produce gcov/.gcov coverage       │
│    reports for selected source files and attach them.                        │
│  • Or rebuild with explicit -fprofile-arcs -ftest-coverage flags or with     │
│    readline enabled after installing headers.                                │
